# Abstract

This gazetteer traces the population histories of 7.371 cities,
urban-type settlements, large rural centres, frontline towns and
regional capitals across Ukraine, Belarus and the Russian Federation
between 2021 and 2026 (Figure and Table ). Organised across the 123
administrative and occupation-history divisions of the war theatre - 33
divisions spanning Ukraine’s 27 constitutional oblasts and
special-status cities, 7 administrative divisions in Belarus and 83
federal subjects in the Russian Federation - the tables pair complete
pre-war census baselines with continuous 2021-2026 war-displacement
trajectories across Ukraine alongside official 2021-2026 Belstat and
2021-2025 Rosstat municipal series.

Across Ukraine, frontline towns in Donetsk, Luhansk, Kharkiv,
Zaporizhzhia and Kherson Oblasts suffered near-total depopulation under
artillery and aerial bombardment, while Kyiv, Dnipro, Lviv and western
regional centres expanded or stabilised as reception hubs for millions
of internally displaced citizens. In Belarus and the Russian Federation,
urban populations reflected the contrast between demographic stability
in Moscow, Saint Petersburg, hydrocarbon boomtowns and major
defence-industrial hubs, evacuation losses in shelled border towns such
as Shebekino and Sudzha and steady contraction across peripheral
garrison and mono-industrial towns.

# Methodology

With Ukraine’s planned 2023 census suspended under martial law and
granular municipal series curtailed in Russian statistical releases
after 2022, complete 2021-2022 census baselines across 2.274 Ukrainian,
306 Belarusian and 5.606 Russian settlements are paired with continuous
2022-2026 war-displacement trajectories, Belstat municipal registers and
published 2023-2025 Rosstat series
[\[1\]](#ref-libanova2024demographic)[\[2\]](#ref-iom2025returns)[\[3\]](#ref-belstat2024chislennost)[\[4\]](#ref-rosstat2024demografiya)[\[5\]](#ref-mediazona2025casualties).
Three razed frontline towns drop to zero inhabitants by 2026, leaving
8.183 inhabited settlements at the 2026 benchmark. Every one of the 123
regional divisions is covered completely.

For Ukrainian settlements, pre-invasion 2021 baselines from the State
Statistics Service of Ukraine are updated through 2022, 2024 and 2026
using Oblast Military Administration evacuation logs, IOM municipal
displacement and returnee series, Copernicus Sentinel-1 radar and UNOSAT
optical building-damage surveys and VIIRS night-time light series
[\[6\]](#ref-ukrstat2022chiselnist)[\[7\]](#ref-acled2025ukraine)[\[8\]](#ref-deepstate2025map)[\[9\]](#ref-scher2023sar)[\[10\]](#ref-unosat2023mariupol).
For Belarusian and Russian cities, municipal baselines from Belstat and
the 2021 All-Russian Census are adjusted for wartime emigration, border
evacuations in Belgorod and Kursk Oblasts, defence-industrial labour
inflows and garrison casualty attrition.

| Country      | Divisions | Settlements | 2021    | 2026    | Change |
|:-------------|:----------|:------------|:--------|:--------|:-------|
| Ukraine      | 37        | 2274        | 36,56M  | 32,13M  | -12,1% |
| Belarus      | 8         | 306         | 8,75M   | 8,57M   | -2%    |
| Russia       | 83        | 5606        | 137,19M | 135,34M | -1,3%  |
| Total Domain | 123       | 8186        | 182,5M  | 176,04M | -3,5%  |

Regional distribution and combined population of the 7.371 catalogued
census settlements across Ukraine, Belarus and the Russian Federation
from 2021 to 2026 (7.369 inhabited in 2026).

# Results

## Ukrainian Oblasts and Occupation Zones

In Ukraine, urban trajectories between 2021 and 2026 diverged sharply
along the line of contact (Table ). Frontline cities and towns subjected
to siege and protracted artillery battles - including Bakhmut, Avdiivka,
Popasna, Vuhledar, Marinka, Vovchansk and Kupiansk - lost over 85% to
99% of their pre-war inhabitants, while Mariupol, Sievierodonetsk,
Lysychansk and Kherson City contracted to a fraction of their 2021
populations. Conversely, central and western regional capitals such as
Kyiv, Dnipro, Poltava, Vinnytsia, Lviv, Ivano-Frankivsk, Chernivtsi and
Uzhhorod absorbed large inflows of internally displaced residents.

Coordinates and resident population trajectories for 2274 Ukrainian
cities and frontline towns across 33 oblast and occupation-history
divisions, 2021-2026.

## Belarusian Oblasts and Minsk City

Across the six oblasts of Belarus and Minsk City, urban populations
experienced gradual contraction or stagnation between 2021 and 2026
(Table ). Natural decrease and skilled emigration to Poland and
Lithuania weighed on Minsk, Gomel, Mogilev, Vitebsk and Bobruisk, while
southern staging towns in Brest and Gomel Oblasts saw modest outflows
following the 2022 northern offensive.

Coordinates and resident population trajectories for 306 Belarusian
regional capitals and industrial centres across all 7 administrative
divisions, 2021-2026.

## Russian Federal Subjects

Across the 83 federal subjects of the Russian Federation, urban
population changes between 2021 and 2026 reflected border bombardment,
defence-industrial mobilisation and regional demographic differentials
(Table ). Shelled frontier towns in Belgorod and Kursk Oblasts
(Shebekino and Sudzha) lost substantial shares of their civilian
residents, whereas Kursk City absorbed displaced border villagers,
hydrocarbon centres in Khanty-Mansi and Yamalo-Nenets Autonomous Okrugs
(Surgut, Nizhnevartovsk and Novy Urengoy) and North Caucasian capitals
(Makhachkala, Grozny and Nazran) recorded steady growth and northern
garrison and mining towns continued to contract.
